# Lint rule candidates for `@bje/eslint-plugin-claude`

This document is the output of the rule spike for #223. It lists each check on a Claude Code
configuration file that the docs make measurable. Each check reads files in a git repository and
needs no model judgment. [ADR 005](adr/005-eslint-plugin-for-claude-config.md) records the
decision to build the plugin. This document supplies its rule inventory.

The list is a candidate set, not a commitment. A rule enters the plugin in its own pull request,
with fixtures that show it reports and stays silent when it must.

## Summary

- **534 candidate rules** in 9 groups. 397 rules go in `recommended`: 242 at
  `error` and 155 at `warn`. 4 rules go in `bje`. 133 rules ship `off`, and `strict` turns them on.
- **Silent failure is the largest category.** 157 rules have the category `no-op`. The file loads,
  but Claude Code ignores a key, a hook or a whole file with no error. 97 more rules catch a
  file that fails to load. A lint rule is the only feedback for most of these.
- **`claude plugin validate` covers little of this.** 48 rules are fully `covered` and
  6 are `partial`. Validate runs only on plugin and marketplace directories. It does not
  read `.claude/` in a project, settings, `.mcp.json`, `.lsp.json` or CLAUDE.md.
- **Every rule cites the docs.** Each row links to the page and heading that source it.

## Method

1. A snapshot of the Claude Code docs was taken on 2026-09-29, at Claude Code 2.1.284. It holds
   211 pages from `code.claude.com/docs/llms.txt`, and the three Agent Skills pages on
   `platform.claude.com`.
2. Eight research passes read the pages by area: skills and commands, subagents and output
   styles, hooks, plugins and marketplaces, memory and context, settings and permissions, MCP and
   tools, and the changelog and errors pages. They recorded about 1,415 findings, each with a
   verbatim quote.
3. Nine consolidation passes grouped the findings by file type into rules and merged the
   duplicates. Each rule got a preset and a severity by the policy below.
4. A script turned each citation into a docs URL and checked that the heading exists in the
   snapshot. All 616 citations resolve.

What is out of scope:

- A check that needs a model judgment about prose quality. A heuristic proxy for one, such as a
  leading imperative verb, is in the list at `off` and is marked "(heuristic)".
- Runtime behavior that no committed file shows.
- A check that only this repository needs, such as the flow directories. Those stay in
  `tests/repo/` (ADR 005, Decision 2).

### Retest: `--plugin-dir` keeps the `src/lib` symlink

This repository's `CLAUDE.md` said that `claude --plugin-dir` drops the `src/lib` symlink. ADR 001
records that the plugins reference once said so. The current docs say that `--plugin-dir` loads the
plugin in place and does not copy it[^plugins-loading-in-place-and-copied-plugins]. They name only
a local path install and a `command` source in copy mode as methods that skip links out of the
plugin[^plugins-host-marketplace-share-files-within-a-marketplace-with-symlinks].

A retest on 2026-09-29 confirms the current docs:

- **Environment:** Claude Code 2.1.284 on Linux, node 22.22.2, in a cloud container. The run started
  in an empty git repository outside this repository, so that no project settings applied.
- **Command:** `claude -p "reply with the word ok" --max-turns 1 --plugin-dir
  /home/user/claude/plugins/gh --debug-file debug.log`.
- **Result:** the debug log shows "Loaded inline plugin from path: gh" at the source path, with no
  copy. The `gh` `SessionStart` hook ran `hooks/hook-io.ts`. That file has a static import of
  `../src/lib/node-floor.ts` and dynamic imports of `src/lib/streams.ts` and `src/lib/gh.ts`. The
  hook returned its normal JSON. A missing link would stop node with a module error before any
  output.

Limits of the retest:

- It covers 2.1.284 only. An older version can behave as the old docs said.
- It covers `--plugin-dir` only. The docs and `CLAUDE.md` agree on the other two methods, so they
  were not retested.
- It covers Linux only. A symlink in a Windows clone needs `core.symlinks`, which is a separate
  constraint.

This pull request corrects `CLAUDE.md` and `plugins/workstreams/README.md` to match. ADR 001 and
RFC 001 are point-in-time records and stay as written.

## How the rules are organized

### Groups follow the file type

Plugins that lint one file format per domain use a rule-name prefix for each domain. Examples are
`eslint-plugin-react` (`jsx-*`) and `@eslint/markdown`. This plugin does the same. Each group
below covers one kind of configuration file, and each rule name starts with its file-type prefix.

| Group | Prefixes | Files |
|-------|----------|-------|
| [Skills and commands](#skills-and-commands) | `skill-`, `command-` | `SKILL.md` and skill directories, legacy `commands/*.md` |
| [Subagents and output styles](#subagents-and-output-styles) | `agent-`, `output-style-` | Subagent `.md` files, output style `.md` files, agent teams keys |
| [Hooks](#hooks) | `hooks-` | `hooks/hooks.json`, settings `hooks`, frontmatter `hooks`, hook scripts |
| [Plugin manifest and layout](#plugin-manifest-and-layout) | `plugin-` | `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, plugin layout, `bin/`, symlinks, `userConfig`, monitors, eval suites |
| [Marketplace manifest](#marketplace-manifest) | `marketplace-`, `settings-` | `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, marketplace and plugin keys in settings |
| [CLAUDE.md, rules and memory](#claudemd-rules-and-memory) | `claude-md-`, `rules-`, `memory-` | CLAUDE.md, CLAUDE.local.md, AGENTS.md, `@imports`, `.claude/rules/`, agent `MEMORY.md` |
| [Settings](#settings) | `settings-`, `keybindings-`, `statusline-` | `.claude/settings*.json` keys, `env`, `keybindings.json`, status line |
| [Permissions and sandbox](#permissions-and-sandbox) | `permissions-`, `sandbox-` | `permissions.*`, `sandbox.*`, `autoMode`, rule syntax in `allowed-tools` and `tools` |
| [MCP and LSP servers](#mcp-and-lsp-servers) | `mcp-`, `lsp-` | `.mcp.json`, `.lsp.json`, plugin `mcpServers` and `lspServers`, MCP settings keys |

### Categories follow intent

`eslint-plugin-import` also sorts its rules by intent ("Static analysis", "Helpful warnings",
"Style guide"). Each rule here has one category of this kind. The category also sets the ESLint
`meta.type`.

| Category | `meta.type` | Meaning | Rules |
|----------|-------------|---------|-------|
| `load` | `problem` | The file or component fails to load, or Claude Code shows an error. | 97 |
| `no-op` | `problem` | The config loads, but Claude Code ignores it with no error. | 157 |
| `security` | `problem` | Committed config that grants access, runs a command or leaks a secret. | 49 |
| `consistency` | `problem` | Two files or entries disagree, collide or shadow each other. | 41 |
| `portability` | `problem` | The config fails on one OS, one version range or with one other setting. | 47 |
| `limit` | `problem` | A size, count or length limit, or a truncation. | 23 |
| `deprecated` | `suggestion` | A legacy or renamed form that still works. | 15 |
| `practice` | `suggestion` | A documented recommendation. Nothing breaks. | 105 |

## Presets and severity

ADR 005 Decision 2 names three configs:

- `recommended` holds the rules that follow from the Claude Code docs, for any team.
- `strict` extends `bje`, then turns on at `warn` each rule that is still off. So every one of
  the 534 rules is on. A rule keeps its `bje` or `recommended` severity where one is set.
  Use `strict` to test the full set, not as a CI gate.
- `bje` extends `recommended` with the conventions of this enterprise.

A rule outside `recommended` and `bje` ships `off`, and only `strict` turns it on.

The Preset column in each rule table names the first config that turns the rule on. The
Proposed `bje` preset section lists the changes that `bje` makes to `recommended` rules.

The boundary between `recommended` and `bje` is firm. A rule goes in `recommended` only when Anthropic
documentation is its source. A preference of this enterprise never goes in `recommended`, even
when it is a good practice. It goes in `bje`.

Severity follows the documented impact:

| Impact in the docs | Preset | Severity |
|--------------------|--------|----------|
| The file or component fails to load, or Claude Code shows an error. | `recommended` | `error` |
| Claude Code ignores the key or file with no error (`no-op`). | `recommended` | `error` |
| Security: the config grants access or leaks a secret. | `recommended` | `error` |
| Security: the config only weakens a default. | `recommended` | `warn` |
| Deprecated or legacy, and still works (for example `commands/` in place of `skills/`). | `recommended` | `warn` |
| A size limit, a truncation or a context cost. | `recommended` | `warn` |
| A documented best practice. | `recommended` | `warn` |
| It fails only on one OS, one version range or with one other setting. | `recommended` | `warn` |
| A heuristic proxy. It can report a false positive. | `strict` only | `warn` |

A `no-op` is `error` because the author wrote config that does nothing, and nothing else tells
them. Legacy `commands/` is `warn`: those files still load. A dropped symlink or a missing hook
script is `error`: the plugin then does not work.

A few rules differ from the table on purpose. Each one says why in its group's notes.

### Counts

| Group | `recommended` error | `recommended` warn | `bje` | `off` | Total |
|-------|--------------------:|-------------------:|------:|------:|------:|
| Skills and commands | 17 | 17 | 0 | 21 | 55 |
| Subagents and output styles | 18 | 14 | 0 | 12 | 44 |
| Hooks | 20 | 17 | 3 | 20 | 60 |
| Plugin manifest and layout | 45 | 24 | 1 | 10 | 80 |
| Marketplace manifest | 33 | 18 | 0 | 4 | 55 |
| CLAUDE.md, rules and memory | 12 | 9 | 0 | 16 | 37 |
| Settings | 23 | 24 | 0 | 16 | 63 |
| Permissions and sandbox | 38 | 20 | 0 | 23 | 81 |
| MCP and LSP servers | 36 | 12 | 0 | 11 | 59 |
| **Total** | **242** | **155** | **4** | **133** | **534** |

The `Validate` column in each table says whether `claude plugin validate` already reports the
check for plugins: `covered`, `partial` or `–`. A `covered` rule stays in the list, because
validate does not run in the editor or on project `.claude/` files.

## Suggested first rules

The first pull request of #223 ships two or three rules. These rules are exact, catch a silent
failure, need only one file each, and have a local fixture:

1. `skill-description-max-length` (Skills). A plain limit on one frontmatter field. It exercises
   `@eslint/markdown` with YAML frontmatter.
2. `command-legacy-format` (Skills). A file-path rule with no parse. It proves the `warn` path.
3. `hooks-event-name-known` (Hooks). An enum check on `hooks.json` and settings. It exercises
   `@eslint/json`.

## Decisions

Decisions on the open questions, made in review of this document:

1. **`skill-name-matches-dir` goes in `bje`.** The docs allow a `name` that differs from the
   directory name, so the rule is an enterprise convention.
2. **`--plugin-dir` keeps the `src/lib` symlink.** The retest in the Method section shows this.
   `CLAUDE.md` and the workstreams README now match.
3. **`plugin-manifest-version-present` is not in `recommended`, and is `error` in `bje`.** The hosting
   docs recommend no `version`, so that users track commits. `--strict` is a validator mode, not a
   recommendation. This repository runs `--strict` in CI, so `bje` enforces it.
   `marketplace-version-duplicate` stays in `recommended`: a `version` in both files is a defect.
4. **A `strict` config turns on every rule.** It extends `bje`, then turns on at `warn` each rule
   that is still off. It is little work and gives an easy way to test. ADR 005 Decision 2 now names three
   configs. Other plugins call such a config `all`, so the name can change.
5. **CI lints without `--cache`.** 82 rules read a second file. The ESLint cache stores one
   result per file, so it does not see a change to that second file. CI lints every file on each
   run, so it catches the defect. The editor and the lefthook step can miss it until CI runs.
   ADR 005 Decision 7 records this. The logic stays simple: each rule reads what it needs, and
   no rule reports on two files.

## Open questions

1. **Settings key scope drifts.** `settings-key-scope` needs a list of the scope of each key. The
   docs say the JSON schema can lag behind releases. The rule needs a data file that we refresh
   from `settings-reference` on each Claude Code upgrade.
2. **Snapshot drift.** These rules come from 2.1.284. The changelog changed several limits over
   time (for example the skill listing budget: 2% in 2.1.32, 1% now). `stale_after` is set for
   six months.

## Rules by group

### Skills and commands

Rules for skill directories and their `SKILL.md` files (project, nested, plugin `skills/`, and a plugin-root `SKILL.md`), their supporting files, and legacy command files (`.claude/commands/**/*.md`, `<plugin>/commands/**/*.md`). 55 rules: 17 error and 17 warn in `recommended`, 21 heuristic or opt-in rules `off`.

Target shorthand: SKILL = `**/.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `<plugin>/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `<plugin>/SKILL.md`. CMD = `**/.claude/commands/**/*.md`, `<plugin>/commands/**/*.md`. A rule applies to SKILL only unless its Checks cell names CMD.

| Rule | Checks | Preset | Severity | Category | Validate | Docs |
|------|--------|--------|----------|----------|----------|------|
| `skill-frontmatter-position` | SKILL, CMD: when the file has a `---` block, the opening `---` is line 1, with no blank line or text before it. Otherwise the whole file, markers included, is skill content and no field is read. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^skills-frontmatter-reference] [^glossary-frontmatter] |
| `skill-frontmatter-valid-yaml` | SKILL, CMD: the YAML between the `---` markers parses. If not, the skill loads with no fields set, so Claude cannot match its `description`. validate covers plugin dirs and a bare `.claude/skills` dir (v2.1.233+). | recommended | error | no-op | covered | [^skills-frontmatter-reference] [^skills-skill-not-triggering] [^changelog-claude-code-changelog] |
| `skill-frontmatter-schema` | SKILL, CMD: keys are in the frontmatter table; near-miss keys (`allowed_tools`, `when-to-use`) get the fix; CMD has no `name` or `paths`. Types and enums: `effort` `low`..`max`; `context` `fork`; `shell` `bash`\|`powershell`; `metadata` map; `compatibility` <= 500 chars; `model` `inherit`, alias or `claude-*`; string or list for `arguments`, `allowed-tools`, `disallowed-tools`, `paths`; `name`, `description`, `argument-hint` strings. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^skills-frontmatter-reference] [^skills-create-your-first-skill] [^claude-directory-frontmatter-fields-by-file] [^errors-model-is-not-a-recognized-model-id] [^changelog-claude-code-changelog] |
| `skill-fork-fields-require-context` | SKILL, CMD: `agent` and `background` are set only with `context: fork`. Without it, both have no effect. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^skills-frontmatter-reference] |
| `skill-agent-exists` | SKILL, CMD: `agent` names a built-in agent (`Explore`, `Plan`, `general-purpose`), a `name` in `.claude/agents/**/*.md`, or a plugin agent. Option `allow` lists user-level agents the repo cannot see. (cross-file) | recommended | error | load | – | [^skills-example-research-skill-using-explore-agent] |
| `skill-invocation-unreachable` | SKILL: not both `disable-model-invocation: true` and `user-invocable: false`. The first blocks Claude, the second blocks the user, so nobody can invoke the skill. | recommended | error | load | – | [^skills-control-who-invokes-a-skill] [^skills-frontmatter-reference] |
| `skill-paths-glob-valid` | SKILL: each `paths` glob is valid. An unescaped `[` forms a bracket expression. Brace expansion of the whole list stays within `maxExpanded` 1,000 patterns and `maxBytes` 4 MiB. A failing pattern matches nothing. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^skills-frontmatter-reference] [^memory-path-specific-rules] [^changelog-claude-code-changelog] |
| `skill-file-layout` | Each directory under `.claude/skills/` or `<plugin>/skills/` holds a file named exactly `SKILL.md`. No loose `.md` file sits directly in `skills/` (such as `.claude/skills/name.md`). Neither form is discovered. | recommended | error | load | – | [^skills-create-your-first-skill] [^plugins-components-skills] [^debug-your-config-check-common-causes] |
| `skill-reserved-name` | Outside a plugin: no skill folder named `synced` (any case); no skill folder, frontmatter `name`, or CMD file or subfolder named `anthropic-skills` or starting `anthropic-skills:`. Claude Code skips each one. | recommended | error | load | – | [^skills-create-your-first-skill] [^skills-when-a-synced-skill-name-matches-another-command] |
| `skill-name-unique` | In one scope (repo `.claude/`, one plugin), effective command names are unique: frontmatter `name`, else directory name; CMD path with `/` as `:`. Compare ignoring case, spacing, invisible chars, fullwidth and dash variants. A command that shares a skill's name never runs. (cross-file) | recommended | error | consistency | – | [^skills-resolve-skills-that-share-a-name] [^skills-how-a-skill-gets-its-command-name] [^skills-when-a-synced-skill-name-matches-another-command] |
| `skill-plugin-root-shadowed` | A `<plugin>/SKILL.md` exists only when the plugin has no `skills/` directory and `plugin.json` has no `skills` key. Otherwise the root file does not load. (cross-file) | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^plugins-components-skills] |
| `skill-plugin-vars-outside-plugin` | Non-plugin SKILL and CMD bodies and `allowed-tools` do not use `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` or `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}`. They are substituted only in plugin skills and stay literal text elsewhere. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^skills-available-string-substitutions] |
| `skill-inject-bang-position` | SKILL, CMD body: an inline `` !`cmd` `` placeholder starts a line or follows whitespace. After any other character (`` KEY=!`cmd` ``) it stays literal text and the command does not run. Ignore fenced code. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^skills-inject-dynamic-context] |
| `skill-reference-exists` | SKILL: each relative Markdown link or backticked relative path in the body resolves to a file inside the skill directory. | recommended | error | load | – | [^skills-add-supporting-files] |
| `skill-allowed-tools-syntax` | SKILL, CMD: each `allowed-tools` and `disallowed-tools` entry is a known tool name, `mcp__` name, or valid permission rule such as `Bash(git add *)`. The syntax rule itself is defined by the `permissions` group; this rule applies it here. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^skills-frontmatter-reference] [^skills-pre-approve-tools-for-a-skill] |
| `skill-allowed-tools-ineffective` | SKILL, CMD: `disallowed-tools` does not list `EndConversation` (not removable while any other tool remains). `allowed-tools` does not list `AskUserQuestion` or other interactive tools (never auto-allowed). | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^skills-frontmatter-reference] [^changelog-claude-code-changelog] |
| `skill-allowed-tools-broad` | Committed SKILL, CMD: `allowed-tools` has no unscoped grant: bare `Bash`, `Bash(*)`, `PowerShell`, `Write`, `Edit`, `WebFetch`, or an `mcp__` wildcard. The grant applies without workspace trust, even in `-p`. Option `allow`. | recommended | error | security | – | [^skills-pre-approve-tools-for-a-skill] |
| `skill-no-bom` | SKILL, CMD: the file does not start with a UTF-8 BOM. Before v2.1.239, Claude Code silently ignored such a file. Fails only below that version (option `minVersion`). | recommended | warn | portability | – | [^changelog-claude-code-changelog] |
| `skill-frontmatter-present` | Plugin SKILL and CMD files have a `---` frontmatter block. Without one, the first non-empty body line becomes the description. validate warns `No frontmatter block found`; `--strict` fails. | recommended | warn | practice | covered | [^skills-frontmatter-reference] [^plugins-components-skills] |
| `skill-boolean-literal` | SKILL, CMD: `disable-model-invocation`, `user-invocable` and `background` use `true` or `false`. `yes`,`no`,`on`,`off`,`1`,`0` work only on v2.1.218 or later (option `minVersion`). | recommended | warn | portability | – | [^skills-frontmatter-reference] |

| `skill-no-time-sensitive` | SKILL and bundled `.md` files: no dated conditional (`before August 2025`, `after <date>`) outside an "Old patterns" section. (heuristic) | off | warn | practice | – | [^platform-agent-skills-best-practices-avoid-time-sensitive-information] |
| `skill-descriptive-filenames` | Files in a skill directory do not have generic names matched by `deny` (default `^(doc\|file\|misc\|untitled)\d*\.`), such as `doc2.md`. (heuristic) | off | warn | practice | – | [^platform-agent-skills-best-practices-runtime-environment] |
