#### Settings: notes
- **No `claude plugin validate` overlap.** No page says validate reads settings, env, statusline, or keybindings files. Every rule here has Validate `–`. The runtime reports some of these at startup (Settings Error, Settings Warning, `/status`, `claude doctor`, keybindings load warnings) [^settings-fix-a-broken-settings-file] [^keybindings-validation].
- **Own key catalog.** The SchemaStore schema "can lag behind the newest CLI releases", so `settings-schema` uses Appendix A.1, not the schema alone [^settings-edit-a-settings-file].
- **Scope needs per-key value exceptions.** `bashEditDiffEnabled` is `User or managed`, but a project `false` still applies. `autoContinueAtUsageLimit` is `User or managed`, but a project value turns the feature off, so it has its own warn rule. `syncClaudeAiSkills`, `syncClaudeAiPlugins`, and `useAutoModeDuringPlan` honor only `false` [^settings-exceptions-to-managed-settings-precedence] [^settings-reference-basheditdiffenabled] [^settings-reference-autocontinueatusagelimit].
- **Scope rule subsumes other groups' per-key scope rows.** The permissions, MCP, marketplace, and memory findings each have per-key "managed only" or "user or managed" rows (e.g. `sandbox-strictallowlist-scope`, `managed-mcp-servers-managed-only`, `claudemd-key-managed-only`, `settings-plugin-managed-only-keys`). As instructed, `settings-key-scope` owns all of them. The other groups keep only the value checks.
- **Conflict, `askUserQuestionTimeout`.** tools-reference.md lists `60s`, `5m`, `10m`. settings-reference.md also lists `never`. This is a subset, not a conflict, so the schema keeps all four. The key is `User or managed`, so project files are flagged by scope anyway [^settings-reference-askuserquestiontimeout] [^tools-reference-question-auto-continue-timeout].
- **Conflict, `bashOutputMaxChars`.** tools-reference.md says "up to 128,000". settings-reference.md clamps to 4000 to 128000. Chose the stricter clamp range [^settings-reference-bashoutputmaxchars] [^tools-reference-output-limits].
- **2 MiB cap.** The docs state it for `--settings` files only. `settings-file-size` applies it to every settings file, because one number covers both and a real file is far smaller [^errors-settings-file-exceeds-the-2mib-limit].
- **`cmd` modifier.** keybindings.md lists `cmd` as a modifier that most terminals do not send. The v2.1.283 changelog removed the claim that `cmd` is an alias of `meta`. `keybindings-portability` warns on it and does not treat it as unknown [^keybindings-modifiers] [^changelog-claude-code-changelog].
- **Runtime-set env vars (A.3).** The docs say Claude Code sets these itself. They do not say that a settings value is dropped. The rule reports them under `settings-env-ignored-var` as values with no purpose; a reviewer may move them to an off rule [^env-vars-variables].
- **Delegated to other groups (not duplicated here).** Hooks: `settings-hooks-shape`, `settings-allowedhttphookurls-*`, `settings-httphookallowedenvvars-type`, `settings-hook-command-project-dir`, the hooks half of `settings-disableallhooks-with-hooks`, `scripts-deprecated-cli-flags` (hook and statusline commands). Permissions: all `perm-*`, `sandbox-*`, `mode-*`, `automode-*`, `settings-defaultmode-*`, `settings-automode-*`, `settings-permissions-known-keys`, `settings-invalid-entry-skipped`, `managed-deny-invalid-withholds-allow`. MCP: `settings-allowedmcpservers-*`, `settings-mcp*`, `settings-enableallprojectmcpservers-shared`, `mcp-*` env-free rows, and `mcpServers` placed in `settings.json`. Marketplace: `settings-enabledplugins-*`, `settings-extraknownmarketplaces-*`, `settings-marketplace-*`, `settings-strict*`, `settings-owner-wildcard-only-in-policy`, `managed-hostpattern-regex-compiles`, `managed-strictplugin-typo`. Memory: `claudeMdExcludes`, `claudeMd`, `autoMemoryDirectory`, `autoMemoryEnabled`, `includeGitInstructions`, `pluginConfigs["agents-md@builtin"]` value checks. Agents and output styles: `outputstyle-plugin-force-overrides`, `agent-memory-vs-automemory-off`. Plugin: `subagentstatusline-plugin-managed-hooks` and plugin `settings.json` keys.

### Permissions and sandbox

Rules for `permissions.*`, `disableAutoMode`, `ignorePatterns`, `autoMode`, and `sandbox.*` in any `.claude/settings*.json` or committed user or managed settings file, plus the shared permission-rule grammar. 82 rules: 59 in `recommended` (39 error, 20 warn) and 23 `off`.

The grammar rules (`permissions-rule-syntax`, `permissions-unknown-tool`, `permissions-tool-name-glob`, `permissions-specifier-unsupported`, `permissions-path-rule-tool`, `permissions-param-rule`) also apply to every other place a permission rule appears: skill `allowed-tools` / `disallowed-tools`, agent `tools` / `disallowedTools`, and hook `if`. The skills, agents, and hooks groups reference them by name.

| Rule | Checks | Preset | Severity | Category | Validate | Docs |
|------|--------|--------|----------|----------|----------|------|
| `permissions-schema` | `permissions` keys only `allow`, `ask`, `deny`, `additionalDirectories`, `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories`, `defaultMode`, `disableBypassPermissionsMode`, `disableAutoMode`. `allow`/`ask`/`deny`/`additionalDirectories` are string arrays; no entry holds a NUL byte. In managed files, a bad `deny`/`ask` list also withholds `allow`. | recommended | error | load | – | [^settings-reference-permissions] [^settings-fix-a-broken-settings-file] [^managed-settings-keys-that-fail-closed] [^changelog-claude-code-changelog] |
| `permissions-default-mode-value` | `permissions.defaultMode` is one of `default`, `acceptEdits`, `plan`, `auto`, `dontAsk`, `bypassPermissions`, `manual`. | recommended | error | load | – | [^settings-reference-permissions-defaultmode] |
| `permissions-disable-mode-value` | `permissions.disableBypassPermissionsMode`, `permissions.disableAutoMode`, and top-level `disableAutoMode` are the string `"disable"` (not `true`). | recommended | error | load | – | [^settings-reference-permissions-disablebypasspermissionsmode] [^settings-reference-disableautomode] |
| `permissions-default-mode-project-ignored` | `defaultMode` `auto` or `bypassPermissions` in `.claude/settings.json` or `.claude/settings.local.json` has no effect. `auto` there also suppresses the user `defaultMode`; `bypassPermissions` starts Manual. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^settings-reference-permissions-defaultmode] [^settings-a-value-you-set-is-ignored] [^changelog-claude-code-changelog] |
| `permissions-bypass-mode-committed` | `defaultMode: "bypassPermissions"` in any committed settings file (project, local, user template, managed). Clients before v2.1.257 honor it from project files. Also reports that the file's `allow` rules have no effect in that mode. | recommended | error | security | – | [^settings-reference-permissions-defaultmode] [^permission-modes-skip-all-checks-with-bypasspermissions-mode] [^permission-modes-available-modes] |
| `permissions-default-mode-conflict` | Same file sets `disableBypassPermissionsMode: "disable"` with `defaultMode: "bypassPermissions"`, or `disableAutoMode: "disable"` (either location) with `defaultMode: "auto"`. The mode is never entered. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^settings-reference-permissions-disablebypasspermissionsmode] [^settings-reference-disableautomode] |
| `permissions-default-mode-manual-alias` | `defaultMode: "manual"` needs Claude Code v2.1.200+; older clients reject it. Option `minVersion`; suggest `default`. | recommended | warn | portability | – | [^settings-reference-permissions-defaultmode] [^permissions-permission-modes] |

#### Permissions and sandbox: notes

- **Scope split with the settings group.** This file owns scope checks for keys under `permissions.*`, `sandbox.*`, `autoMode`, and `disableAutoMode`. The settings group's generic key-scope catalog should skip those prefixes. The other top-level keys in the settings-reference "Permission settings" section (`allowManagedPermissionRulesOnly`, `skipAutoPermissionPrompt`, `skipDangerousModePermissionPrompt`, `useAutoModeDuringPlan`) and permission-adjacent keys (`skipWebFetchPreflight`, `disableAllHooks`, env vars `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_AUTO_MODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_POWERSHELL_CMD_RM_DENY`) stay with settings. [^settings-reference-permissions]
- **Mid-pattern `:*` conflict.** permissions.md says `Bash(git:* push)` treats the colon as literal and never matches. The changelog (v2.1.282) says such rules now work from every source with a startup warning. The stricter reading is kept: `permissions-bash-colon-star-mid` is an error. [^permissions-wildcard-patterns] [^changelog-claude-code-changelog]
- **Non-MCP globs in `allow`.** permissions.md says an unanchored allow glob is skipped with a warning; the changelog (v2.1.166) says allow rules reject non-MCP globs. Both make the rule dead, so `permissions-tool-name-glob` is an error. [^permissions-tool-name-wildcards]
- **Path-rule tool list.** The docs name `Write`, `NotebookEdit`, `Glob`, `MultiEdit` as never consulted. tools-reference maps `Grep` and `LSP` to `Read(...)` too, and lists no specifier format for them, so `permissions-path-rule-tool` also reports `Grep(path)` and `LSP(path)` (stricter). [^tools-reference-configure-tools-with-permission-rules-and-hooks]
- **Two path syntaxes.** `Read`/`Edit`/`Cd` rules: `/x` is relative to the settings source, `//x` absolute. Sandbox filesystem paths: `/x` absolute, `./x` project-relative. Rules must pick the syntax by key. [^permissions-read-and-edit] [^settings-reference-sandbox-path-prefixes]
- **IPv6 spelling.** Domain lists need `[::1]`; `injectHosts` needs bare `::1`. [^sandboxing-ipv6-addresses-in-domain-lists] [^settings-reference-mask-fields-for-environment-variables]
- **`bypassPermissions` in project files** is both a no-op today and honored by clients before v2.1.257, so it fires `permissions-default-mode-project-ignored` and `permissions-bypass-mode-committed`. [^settings-reference-permissions-defaultmode]
- **Mask and AWS rules** only matter in user or managed files (dotfiles repos, IT repos with `managed-settings.json`), because project files drop `mask` entries (`sandbox-scope`). [^settings-reference-sandbox-credentials]
- **The unsandboxed retry.** The docs recommend `allowUnsandboxedCommands: false` for a strict sandbox, but whether a repo wants a strict sandbox is intent, so `sandbox-unsandboxed-retry` is a heuristic. [^sandboxing-enforce-sandboxing-with-managed-settings]
- **`permissions-auto-mode-dropped-allow`** depends on the session mode, which no file fixes (auto is the built-in default from v2.1.283), so it is a conditional warn, not an error. [^permission-modes-when-auto-mode-falls-back]
- **Info-only rows not turned into rules.** These show no wrong file state: project `allow`/`additionalDirectories` wait for trust; bare `WebFetch` versus `domain:*` differ in sandbox effect; `Cd` patterns match whole paths; `Bash` allow rules never reach Cowork's `mcp__workspace__bash`; a `Bash(find *)` rule does not cover `-exec`/`-delete`. [^permissions-project-allow-rules-and-workspace-trust] [^permissions-allow-or-deny-every-fetch] [^permissions-cd] [^permissions-mcp] [^permissions-compound-commands]
- **Validate.** No docs page says `claude plugin validate` checks permission rules or sandbox keys; plugin `settings.json` does not carry them. Validate is `–` for every rule.

### MCP and LSP servers

Covers the root `.mcp.json`, plugin `.mcp.json` and `plugin.json` `mcpServers`, `managed-mcp.json` and `managedMcpServers` when a config repo commits them, `.lsp.json` and `plugin.json` `lspServers`, the MCP keys and MCP `env` variables in `.claude/settings*.json`, and MCP tool names wherever a tool is named. 61 rules, plus an appendix of canonical built-in tool names that the permissions, skills, agents and hooks groups reference.

| Rule | Checks | Preset | Severity | Category | Validate | Docs |
|------|--------|--------|----------|----------|----------|------|
| `mcp-json-location` | An MCP config under `.claude/` (`.claude/.mcp.json`, `.claude/mcp.json`, `.claude/config/mcp.json`) is never read. Project servers go in `.mcp.json` at the repository root. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^debug-your-config-check-common-causes] [^mcp-quickstart-troubleshooting] |
| `mcp-json-servers-key` | Root `.mcp.json` has a top-level `mcpServers` object. Flag VS Code's `servers` key and server entries at the top level. A plugin `.mcp.json` may omit the wrapper. | recommended | error | load | – | [^debug-your-config-check-common-causes] [^mcp-project-scope] [^plugins-components-mcp-servers] |
| `mcp-json-file-size` | `.mcp.json` is a regular file (not a FIFO, device, or link to one), `max` 2,097,152 bytes. Otherwise `claude mcp add`, `add-json --scope project` and `remove` exit with an error. | recommended | error | limit | – | [^errors-cant-read-mcpjson] |
| `mcp-settings-mcpservers` | `.claude/settings*.json` has no `mcpServers` key. Settings files do not read it; define servers in `.mcp.json`. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^debug-your-config-check-common-causes] |
| `mcp-server-schema` | Each server entry is an object. `type` is `stdio`, `http`, `streamable-http`, `sse` or `ws` (absent means stdio). `url` without `type` fails. stdio needs string `command` and string-array `args`. Remote needs `url`, a valid absolute URL. `type: "sdk"` is skipped. A bad entry is dropped. | recommended | error | load | covered (plugin MCP configs, v2.1.281+; not root `.mcp.json`) | [^mcp-option-1-add-a-remote-http-server] [^mcp-quickstart-edit-mcpjson-directly] [^mcp-quickstart-troubleshooting] [^plugins-manifest-reference-validate-the-manifest] [^plugins-troubleshooting-server-is-configured-but-never-connects] |
| `mcp-server-name-reserved` | Server key is not a reserved built-in name: `workspace`, `claude-in-chrome`, `computer-use`, `Claude Preview`, `Claude Browser` (option `names` extends the list). Such a server is skipped at load. | recommended | error | load | covered (plugin MCP configs, as a dropped entry) | [^mcp-configuration-warnings] |
| `mcp-server-name-format` | Server key matches `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$`. `claude mcp` commands and Claude Desktop import reject other names; tool names replace other characters with `_`. | recommended | warn | practice | – | [^mcp-from-an-mcpservers-json-block] [^errors-could-not-import-a-server-from-claude-desktop] [^mcp-plugin-provided-mcp-servers] |
| `mcp-server-name-anthropic-skills` | Server key is not `anthropic-skills`. Claude Code reserves that name for synced skills, so the server's prompts do not appear as commands. Its tools still work. | recommended | warn | consistency | – | [^mcp-use-mcp-prompts-as-commands] |
| `mcp-remote-url-empty` | Root `.mcp.json` only: a remote entry with `url: ""` shows `not configured` and never connects. A plugin placeholder entry is allowed. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^mcp-server-status-detail] |
| `mcp-no-sse-transport` | `type: "sse"` is deprecated. Use `http` where the server supports it. | recommended | warn | deprecated | – | [^mcp-option-2-add-a-remote-sse-server] |
| `mcp-oauth-transport` | An `oauth` object on an entry whose `type` is `stdio`, absent, or `ws` has no effect. OAuth applies to HTTP and SSE only; `ws` auth is header-only. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^mcp-option-4-add-a-remote-websocket-server] [^mcp-use-pre-configured-oauth-credentials] |
| `mcp-oauth-values` | `oauth.authServerMetadataUrl` starts with `https://`. `oauth.scopes` is one space-separated string, not an array. | recommended | error | load | – | [^mcp-override-oauth-metadata-discovery] [^mcp-restrict-oauth-scopes] |
| `mcp-authorization-header-with-oauth` | An entry with a static `headers.Authorization` and an `oauth` object: Claude Code never falls back to OAuth for that server, so `oauth` has no effect. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^mcp-authenticate-with-remote-mcp-servers] [^mcp-use-dynamic-headers-for-custom-authentication] |
| `mcp-hidden-whitespace` | No leading or trailing whitespace in `command`, `url`, each `args` item, or the keys and values of `env` and `headers`. Claude Code uses them as written. | recommended | error | load | – | [^mcp-configuration-warnings] |
| `mcp-timeout-min` | Per-server `timeout` is in milliseconds, `min` 1000. A lower value, such as seconds written as `60`, is ignored and falls through to `MCP_TOOL_TIMEOUT`. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^mcp-push-messages-with-channels] |
| `mcp-project-dir-default` | In root `.mcp.json` `command` or `args`, `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}` needs a default, such as `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-.}`. Plugin configs substitute it directly and are exempt. | recommended | error | load | – | [^mcp-option-3-add-a-local-stdio-server] |
| `mcp-env-expansion-field` | `${VAR}` expands only in `command`, `args`, `env`, `url` and `headers` (plugin path placeholders also in `headersHelper`). A reference in another field, such as `oauth.*` or `timeout`, stays literal. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^mcp-expansion-locations] [^mcp-plugin-provided-mcp-servers] |
| `mcp-credential-var-remote` | A remote `url` or `headers` does not reference a covered credential variable: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `NPM_TOKEN` (option `names` extends). It reads as empty and `:-default` is ignored. | recommended | error | load | – | [^mcp-credential-variables-that-read-as-empty] |
| `mcp-headershelper-credential-env` | Root or plugin `.mcp.json`: an inline `headersHelper` does not read a variable whose name contains `TOKEN`, `SECRET`, `PASSWORD`, `KEY` or `AUTH` (any case, except `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_<n>`). Claude Code removes them. | recommended | error | load | – | [^mcp-which-variables-a-helper-can-read] |
| `mcp-headershelper-path` | `headersHelper` names an absolute path or a bare command on `PATH`, not a relative path. Its working directory depends on where the server is configured. | recommended | warn | practice | – | [^mcp-use-dynamic-headers-for-custom-authentication] |
| `mcp-headershelper-committed` | Flags each `headersHelper` in a committed root `.mcp.json`. It is an arbitrary shell command that runs once a user trusts the folder. | recommended | warn | security | – | [^mcp-trust-a-folder-before-its-headershelper-runs] |
| `mcp-stdio-relative-path` | Root `.mcp.json`: a `./` or `../` file path in `command` or `args` resolves against the launch directory, not `.mcp.json`. Fails when a user launches from a subdirectory. Use `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-.}/...`. | recommended | warn | portability | – | [^debug-your-config-check-mcp-servers] [^channels-reference-example-build-a-webhook-receiver] |
| `mcp-anthropic-hosted-url` | `url` host is not an Anthropic-hosted connector host: `microsoft365.mcp.claude.com`, `gmail.mcp.claude.com`, `gcal.mcp.claude.com` (option `hosts` extends). Claude Code refuses local OAuth for them. | recommended | error | load | – | [^errors-server-is-anthropic-hosted-and-doesnt-support-local-oauth] |
| `mcp-insecure-url` | A remote `url` does not use `http://` or `ws://` to a non-loopback host. | recommended | warn | security | covered (warning, plugin MCP configs) | [^plugins-manifest-reference-validate-the-manifest] |
| `mcp-plugin-bundle-extension` | `plugin.json` `mcpServers`: a string item that is not a `.json` path is a bundle path or `https://` URL that ends in `.mcpb` or `.dxt`. | recommended | error | load | covered | [^plugins-manifest-reference-mcpservers] [^plugins-components-include-a-packaged-mcpb-server] |
| `mcp-project-plugin-bundle` | A plugin under `.claude/skills/` does not declare an MCP server as a `.mcpb`/`.dxt` bundle or from a file outside the plugin directory. Those servers are skipped. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^plugins-loading-plugins-shared-through-a-repository] |
| `mcp-duplicate-server-name` | (cross-file) One plugin declares a server name once across `.mcp.json`, each `mcpServers` `.json` file and the inline map. A later declaration replaces an earlier one. | recommended | error | consistency | – | [^plugins-components-mcp-servers] [^plugins-manifest-reference-mcpservers] |
| `mcp-plugin-stdio-reach` | A plugin stdio server runs in Claude Code and local Cowork only, not on claude.ai. Use a remote `https://` server to reach claude.ai users. Applies when the plugin targets claude.ai. | recommended | warn | portability | – | [^plugins-components-reach-users-on-claudeai-and-cowork] |
| `mcp-plugin-tool-name-scoped` | (cross-file) In a plugin, a tool reference to its own server (skill `allowed-tools`, agent `tools`/`disallowedTools`, hook `matcher`/`if`) uses `mcp__plugin_<plugin>_<server>__<tool>`, with characters outside `A-Za-z0-9_-` as `_`. `mcp__<server>__` never matches. An `mcp_tool` hook `server` uses `plugin:<plugin>:<server>`. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^mcp-plugin-provided-mcp-servers] [^plugins-components-server-names-tool-names-and-reloads] [^plugins-components-environment-quoting-and-matching-mcp-tools] |
| `mcp-tool-name-format` | A tool reference that starts with `mcp` matches `mcp__<server>` or `mcp__<server>__<tool>`, with a server segment in `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`. Flag `mcp_server_tool`, `mcp-server` and similar forms, which match no tool. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^hooks-match-mcp-tools] [^permissions-mcp] |
| `mcp-tool-server-unknown` | (cross-file, heuristic) `mcp__<server>` in a tool reference names a server in root `.mcp.json`, a plugin MCP config or an agent inline server. User-scope and connector servers are not visible. | off | warn | consistency | – | [^permissions-mcp] |
| `mcp-approval-committed` | Committed `.claude/settings.json` does not set `enableAllProjectMcpServers: true` or `enabledMcpjsonServers`. In a trusted folder they approve repository servers without a prompt; in an untrusted one they are ignored. | recommended | error | security | – | [^settings-reference-enableallprojectmcpservers] [^settings-reference-enabledmcpjsonservers] [^mcp-project-server-approvals-and-workspace-trust] |
| `mcp-approval-names-exist` | (cross-file) Each name in `enabledMcpjsonServers` and `disabledMcpjsonServers` is a key of root `.mcp.json` `mcpServers`. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^settings-reference-enabledmcpjsonservers] [^settings-reference-disabledmcpjsonservers] |
| `mcp-approval-conflict` | (cross-file) A name in both `enabledMcpjsonServers` and `disabledMcpjsonServers` across the repository's settings files is rejected. The enable has no effect. | recommended | error | consistency | – | [^settings-reference-disabledmcpjsonservers] [^mcp-project-server-approvals-and-workspace-trust] |
| `mcp-disable-connectors-false` | `disableClaudeAiConnectors: false` is the same as unset and cannot re-enable connectors that another scope disables. Remove it. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^settings-reference-disableclaudeaiconnectors] [^mcp-disable-claudeai-connectors] |
| `mcp-project-toggle-keys` | (heuristic) `disabledMcpServers` or `enabledMcpServers` in a settings file. These are per-project state in `~/.claude.json`, not the `*McpjsonServers` settings. | off | warn | no-op | – | [^mcp-disable-a-server-without-removing-it] |
| `mcp-policy-entry-schema` | `allowedMcpServers`/`deniedMcpServers` items have exactly one key: `serverName`, `serverCommand` (string array) or `serverUrl`. Allowlist `serverName` matches `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$`; denylist `serverName` is non-empty with no leading or trailing whitespace. `*` in `serverName` is literal. | recommended | error | load | – | [^settings-reference-allowedmcpservers] [^settings-reference-deniedmcpservers] [^managed-mcp-match-servers-by-url-command-or-name] |
| `mcp-policy-servername-weak` | Flags `serverName` policy entries: a name is a user label, not a security control. Also flags allowlist names that stop admitting stdio (remote) servers once any `serverCommand` (`serverUrl`) entry exists. | recommended | warn | security | – | [^managed-mcp-match-servers-by-url-command-or-name] [^settings-reference-allowedmcpservers] |
| `mcp-allowlist-servername-dead` | An allowlist that has both `serverUrl` and `serverCommand` entries: each `serverName` entry never matches. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^managed-mcp-example-configuration] [^managed-mcp-how-a-server-is-evaluated] |
| `mcp-allow-deny-overlap` | (cross-file) An entry in both `allowedMcpServers` and `deniedMcpServers`: the server is blocked and the allow entry is dead. | recommended | error | consistency | – | [^settings-reference-allowedmcpservers] [^settings-reference-deniedmcpservers] |
| `mcp-allowlist-empty` | `allowedMcpServers: []` blocks every server users add, unlike an unset key. Confirm the intent. | recommended | warn | practice | – | [^managed-mcp-match-servers-by-url-command-or-name] [^settings-reference-allowedmcpservers] |
| `mcp-policy-literal-values` | No `${VAR}` in a policy `serverUrl` or `serverCommand`. It depends on the launching shell, and an allowlist entry whose expansion changes scheme, host or path scope is ignored. | recommended | warn | security | – | [^managed-mcp-how-policy-entries-expand] |
| `mcp-managed-servers-entry` | `managedMcpServers` is an object keyed by name, not an array. Name `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$`; `type` `http`, `streamable-http` or `sse`; `https://` `url`; no `command`, `args`, `env`, `headersHelper`; no `${VAR}`; no control or invisible characters. | recommended | error | load | – | [^managed-mcp-what-an-entry-can-contain] [^settings-reference-managedmcpservers] |
| `mcp-env-var-values` | Settings `env`: `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` is `true`, `false`, `auto` or `auto:N` (N 0 to 100); `MCP_SDK_GENERATION` `v1`/`v2`; `MCP_PROTOCOL_NEGOTIATION` `auto`/`legacy`; `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_MCP_DESCRIPTION_LENGTH` positive plain digits. Other values are ignored. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^env-vars-variables] [^mcp-configure-tool-search] |
| `mcp-env-var-numbers` | (heuristic) Settings `env` integer values: milliseconds for `MCP_TIMEOUT`, `MCP_TOOL_TIMEOUT`, `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_TOOL_IDLE_TIMEOUT`, `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_AUTO_BACKGROUND_MS`, `MCP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS`; tokens for `MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS`. | off | warn | practice | – | [^env-vars-variables] [^mcp-mcp-output-limits-and-warnings] |
| `mcp-tool-search-disabled` | Settings `env.ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH` is not `false`. `false` loads every MCP tool definition into context upfront. | recommended | warn | limit | – | [^mcp-configure-tool-search] |
| `mcp-env-client-secret` | Committed settings `env` does not set `MCP_CLIENT_SECRET`, the OAuth client secret for MCP servers. | recommended | error | security | – | [^env-vars-variables] [^mcp-use-pre-configured-oauth-credentials] |
| `mcp-always-load-count` | (heuristic) Servers with `alwaysLoad: true` across the repository's MCP configs, `max` 2 (no documented number). Each loads all its tools upfront and holds startup up to 5 seconds. | off | warn | limit | – | [^mcp-exempt-a-server-from-deferral] |
| `mcp-no-literal-secrets` | (heuristic) No credential-like literal in `headers`, `env`, `args`, `url` userinfo, or an `oauth` secret key of a committed MCP config, including `managed-mcp.json` `env`. Use `${VAR}`. | off | warn | security | covered (warning for header values, plugin MCP configs) | [^mcp-environment-variable-expansion-in-mcpjson] [^mcp-use-pre-configured-oauth-credentials] [^managed-mcp-authenticate-with-per-user-credentials] [^plugins-manifest-reference-validate-the-manifest] |
| `mcp-env-var-syntax` | (heuristic) Only `${VAR}` and `${VAR:-default}` expand. Flag `$VAR`, `%VAR%`, `${VAR-x}` and `${VAR:=x}`, which stay literal. | off | warn | no-op | – | [^mcp-supported-syntax] |
| `mcp-env-var-default` | (heuristic) `${VAR}` with no `:-default` in a committed `.mcp.json`. When the variable is unset, the server loads with the literal `${VAR}` text. | off | warn | practice | – | [^mcp-unset-variables-without-a-default] |
| `mcp-unknown-keys` | (heuristic) Entry keys outside `type`, `command`, `args`, `env`, `url`, `headers`, `headersHelper`, `oauth`, `timeout`, `alwaysLoad`; `oauth` keys outside `clientId`, `callbackPort`, `authServerMetadataUrl`, `scopes`; `callbackPort` an integer 1 to 65535. | off | warn | no-op | – | [^mcp-use-pre-configured-oauth-credentials] [^mcp-option-4-add-a-remote-websocket-server] [^mcp-use-a-fixed-oauth-callback-port] |
| `mcp-stdio-command-has-args` | (heuristic) A stdio `command` with whitespace and no `args`. Put the program in `command` and its arguments in `args`. | off | warn | practice | – | [^mcp-quickstart-edit-mcpjson-directly] |
| `mcp-plugin-root-paths` | (heuristic) Plugin MCP `command`, `args` or `env` reference a bundled file without `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` (a relative or `../` path). | off | warn | portability | – | [^mcp-plugin-provided-mcp-servers] |
| `lsp-json-schema` | `.lsp.json` maps names to configs, no wrapper. Each config has only the 13 documented keys; `command` required, no whitespace unless it starts with `/`; `extensionToLanguage` non-empty, keys start with `.`; `startupTimeout`/`shutdownTimeout` positive integers; `maxRestarts` integer >= 0; booleans typed. One bad entry skips the file. | recommended | error | load | covered for inline `lspServers` only; validate does not read `.lsp.json` | [^plugins-components-lsp-servers] [^plugins-manifest-reference-lspservers] [^plugins-manifest-reference-unrecognized-fields] |
| `lsp-transport-socket` | `transport` is not `socket`. Claude Code accepts it but runs every server over stdio. | recommended | error | no-op | – | [^plugins-manifest-reference-lspservers] |
| `lsp-extension-conflict` | (cross-file) No two LSP servers in the repository's plugins claim the same `extensionToLanguage` key. Only the first registered handles those files. | recommended | error | consistency | – | [^plugins-components-lsp-servers] |
| `lsp-duplicate-server-name` | (cross-file) One plugin declares an LSP server name once across `.lsp.json` and each `lspServers` config. A later declaration replaces an earlier one. | recommended | error | consistency | – | [^plugins-components-lsp-servers] [^plugins-manifest-reference-lspservers] |
| `lsp-json-location` | (heuristic) `.lsp.json` outside a plugin root, such as at a repository root with no plugin or under `.claude/`. Claude Code takes LSP config only from plugins. | off | warn | no-op | – | [^plugins-components-lsp-servers] [^tools-reference-lsp-tool-behavior] |

#### MCP and LSP servers: notes

- Scope of `claude plugin validate`: it checks plugin `.mcp.json`, `mcpServers` `.json` files and inline `mcpServers` on v2.1.281+ (dropped entries, undeclared `${user_config.KEY}`, invalid remote `url` as errors; insecure URL and literal credential header as warnings). It never reads the root `.mcp.json` or `.lsp.json`, and checks `lspServers` paths only on v2.1.283+ [^plugins-manifest-reference-validate-the-manifest] [^plugins-components-lsp-servers].
- The `.mcp.json` rules also apply to a committed `managed-mcp.json`, which "uses the same format as a project `.mcp.json`" [^managed-mcp-deploy-managed-mcpjson]. The agents group reuses `mcp-server-schema` for inline `mcpServers` in agent frontmatter.
- Conflict, relative paths: debug-your-config says relative `command`/`args` paths resolve against the launch directory [^debug-your-config-check-mcp-servers]; channels-reference tells a project `.mcp.json` to use a relative path [^channels-reference-example-build-a-webhook-receiver]. I chose the stricter reading and flag them, as warn, because they fail only on launch from another directory.
- Server-name charset: the docs state it for `claude mcp` commands, pasted JSON blocks and Desktop import, not for a hand-edited `.mcp.json` key [^mcp-from-an-mcpservers-json-block]. So `mcp-server-name-format` is warn practice, not error.
- Partial lists: reserved names ("including"), covered credential variables ("such as") and Anthropic-hosted hosts ("include") are partial. Each rule takes an option to extend its list [^mcp-configuration-warnings] [^mcp-credential-variables-that-read-as-empty].
- `mcp-remote-url-empty` is limited to the root `.mcp.json`, because the docs endorse an empty `url` as a plugin placeholder [^mcp-server-status-detail].
- `mcp-oauth-transport`: the stdio quote names the CLI flags; `add-json` takes the same values "in an `oauth` object", so I treat the object the same [^mcp-use-pre-configured-oauth-credentials].
- `TaskOutput`: tools-reference lists it as deprecated [^tools-reference-tools-reference]; permissions calls it a tool "Claude Code has removed" [^permissions-tool-name-wildcards]. The appendix uses the stricter "removed".
- `Glob`: `Read(path)` rules apply to Glob [^tools-reference-configure-tools-with-permission-rules-and-hooks], but a `Glob(path)` rule is accepted and never consulted [^permissions-read-and-edit]. The appendix records both.
- Overlaps for the caller to dedupe: `mcp-headershelper-user-config` versus the plugin group's shell-field `${user_config}` rule; `mcp-managed-only-keys` versus the settings group's managed-only key rule; `mcp-plugin-tool-name-scoped` versus the hooks group's plugin MCP matcher rule.
- Handed to other groups (read from mcp-lsp-tools.md): permissions (`ToolName(specifier)` syntax, specifier-only tools, `WebFetch(domain:)`, bare `WebSearch`/`SendMessage`/`ListAgents`, `EndConversation` deny, `mcp__` rules with parentheses, unanchored allow globs, unknown tool names); hooks (bare-name matchers, `mcp__<server>__.*`, `mcp_tool` `server` as `plugin:<plugin>:<server>`, `Bash|PowerShell`); settings (`askUserQuestionTimeout`, `bashOutputMaxChars`, tool memory vars, PowerShell vars, `crossSessionInbound`, `isolatePeerMachines`, `channelsEnabled`, `allowedChannelPlugins`, `loop.md`, `scheduled_tasks.json`); plugin (`channels[].server`, eval mock server names, `${user_config}` declarations); skills (`shell: powershell`, `Server:tool` names).
- Headings for two errors.md footnotes are HTML `<h3>` elements, not `#` lines; the text is verbatim.

#### Appendix: canonical built-in tool names

46 names from the tools table, case-sensitive [^tools-reference-tools-reference]. "Perm" is the table's "Permission Required" column. Rule forms come from [^tools-reference-configure-tools-with-permission-rules-and-hooks]; any built-in also takes `Tool(param:value)` in deny and ask rules [^permissions-match-by-input-parameter]. Hook matchers use the bare name only.

| Tool | Perm | Rule form | Notes |
|------|------|-----------|-------|
| `Agent` | No | `Agent(<type>)` | `Task` is the pre-2.1.63 name and still works as an alias [^sub-agents-restrict-which-subagents-can-be-spawned]. |
| `Artifact` | Yes | bare | |
| `AskUserQuestion` | No | bare | |
| `Bash` | Yes | `Bash(<command pattern>)` | |
| `CronCreate` | No | bare | |
| `CronDelete` | No | bare | |
| `CronList` | No | bare | |
| `Edit` | Yes | `Edit(<path>)` | `Edit(path)` also covers Write and NotebookEdit. |
| `EndConversation` | No | bare | Deny and ask rules that name it have no effect [^tools-reference-endconversation-tool-behavior]. |
| `EnterPlanMode` | No | bare | |
| `EnterWorktree` | Yes | bare | |
| `ExitPlanMode` | Yes | bare | |
| `ExitWorktree` | No | bare | |
| `Glob` | No | bare; paths via `Read(<path>)` | Absent by default on macOS, Linux and WSL [^tools-reference-glob-tool-behavior]. A `Glob(path)` rule is never consulted. |
| `Grep` | No | bare; paths via `Read(<path>)` | Absent by default on macOS, Linux and WSL. |
| `ListAgents` | No | bare | Bare name only [^cross-session-messaging-turn-off-cross-session-messaging]. |
| `ListMcpResourcesTool` | No | bare | |
| `LSP` | No | bare; paths via `Read(<path>)` | Inactive until a code intelligence plugin is installed [^tools-reference-lsp-tool-behavior]. |
| `Monitor` | Yes | command pattern, as `Bash` | |
| `NotebookEdit` | Yes | bare; paths via `Edit(<path>)` | A `NotebookEdit(path)` rule is never consulted [^permissions-read-and-edit]. |
| `PowerShell` | Yes | `PowerShell(<command pattern>)` | |
| `PushNotification` | No | bare | |
| `Read` | No | `Read(<path>)` | `Read(path)` also covers Grep, Glob and LSP. |
| `ReadMcpResourceTool` | No | bare | |
| `RemoteTrigger` | No | bare | |
| `ReportFindings` | No | bare | |
| `ScheduleWakeup` | No | bare | |
| `SendFeedback` | No | bare | |
| `SendMessage` | No | bare | Bare name only. |
| `SendUserFile` | No | bare | |
| `ShareOnboardingGuide` | Yes | bare | |
| `Skill` | Yes | `Skill(<name>)` | |
| `SubagentHandback` | No | bare | |
| `TaskCreate` | No | bare | Default only on some models [^tools-reference-task-tool-availability]. |
| `TaskGet` | No | bare | As `TaskCreate`. |
| `TaskList` | No | bare | As `TaskCreate`. |
| `TaskOutput` | No | bare | Deprecated or removed; use `Read` on the output file. Exempt from the unknown-name warning. |
| `TaskStop` | No | bare | UI label "Stop Task" is not a rule name [^permissions-tool-name-wildcards]. |
| `TaskUpdate` | No | bare | As `TaskCreate`. |
| `TodoWrite` | No | bare | Disabled by default in favor of the Task tools. |
| `ToolSearch` | No | bare | Bare deny turns tool search off [^mcp-configure-tool-search]. |
| `WaitForMcpServers` | No | bare | Appears only when tool search is off. |
| `WebFetch` | Yes | `WebFetch(domain:<host>)` | `WebFetch(domain:*)` allows every domain [^tools-reference-webfetch-tool-behavior]. |
| `WebSearch` | Yes | bare only | [^tools-reference-websearch-tool-behavior] |
| `Workflow` | Yes | bare | |
| `Write` | Yes | bare; paths via `Edit(<path>)` | A `Write(path)` rule is never consulted. |

Other names a rule may contain:

- `Task`: legacy alias of `Agent`, still valid [^sub-agents-restrict-which-subagents-can-be-spawned].
- `MultiEdit`: legacy tool; a path rule for it is accepted but never consulted. Use `Edit(path)` [^permissions-read-and-edit].
- `Cd`: permission-rule name for the `/cd` command, not a model tool. Bare or `Cd(<path-pattern>)` [^permissions-cd].
- The advisor tool has no name for rules or matchers [^tools-reference-check-which-tools-are-available].
- MCP forms: `mcp__<server>`, `mcp__<server>__<tool>`, `mcp__<server>__*` (allow globs only after a literal `mcp__<server>__`), `mcp__plugin_<plugin>_<server>__<tool>`, `mcp__claude_ai_<server>__<tool>`; Cowork adds `mcp__workspace__bash` and `mcp__workspace__web_fetch` [^permissions-mcp] [^permissions-tool-name-wildcards] [^mcp-plugin-provided-mcp-servers].
